# Phase 3 complete replacement plan — canonical pack beta hard cut

Status: **Wave 2 revised replacement; ready for an exact `STABLE` check and then a fresh settled-plan wave**.

This plan supersedes the current plan in full. It incorporates every accepted Wave 1 and Wave 2 synthesis item while preserving the complete North Star, frozen goal, authorization boundaries, done criteria, validation contract, and anti-pattern rejections.

## Determination

- Estimated implementation effort: **6.2–8.6 engineer-weeks**, including cumulative reviews and expected rework.
- Huge run: **yes**.
- Difficulty: **5/5**.
- Proposed `[XHARD]` tasks: **none**. The implementation remains decomposable normal Luna work; the Phase 4 oracle makes the final classification.
- New research or exploration lanes: **none**.
- Open planning questions: **none**.
- Execution order: immutable baseline → executable v2 contract/catalog → bundled conversion and resource closure → database hard cut → operational convergence → deletion/audit/packaging → one-build validation and evidence closure.
- The estimate increases from 5.7–8.0 weeks because Wave 2 makes three previously implicit systems concrete: operation-scoped snapshots, the bounded resource classifier, and the reproducible coverage generator. No product scope is added.
- The missing runaway demo input remains a deterministic temporary-project fixture repair unless custody evidence proves it is an intentional packaged asset.

## Mandate and boundaries

Implement one strict canonical pack system:

- Every retained bundled product pack is declared by exactly one `pack.yaml` with integer `schema_version: 2`.
- The empty `builtin` product pack is deleted, leaving **22 retained product packs**, plus irreducible `_core` guidance.
- `timeline`, `shots`, `references`, and `runaway` become ordinary packs with `database` contributions.
- Existing typed registries, migration safety, repositories, `DatabaseWriter`, `UnitOfWork`, static application/service/CLI/bridge factories, SDK behavior, and conformance semantics remain.
- Bundled trust comes exclusively from loader provenance.
- External packs remain capability-only. An external manifest containing `database` is rejected as a whole before resolving its declared resources or reading SQL.
- Every bundled customization is owned by a pack or explicitly classified as irreducible kernel behavior.
- Every user/agent-facing bundled pack has packaged structured documentation.
- No per-project composition state, dependency solver, dynamic database plugin system, marketplace, lifecycle machinery, or generalized service container is added.

The North Star guides implementation but cannot widen the frozen goal.

---

# I. Exact v2 manifest contract

WP1 must land this contract as JSON Schema, normalized immutable Python types, golden fixtures, and invalid fixtures before bundled conversion begins.

## 1. Lexical types

| Type | Exact rule |
|---|---|
| `PackId` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` |
| `ReleaseVersion` | `^(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)\.(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)\.(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)$`; no prerelease/build suffix |
| `LowerIdent` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` |
| `QualifiedId` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` |
| `DottedVocabulary` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*)+$` |
| `RepositoryId` | `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$` |
| `MigrationName` | `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` |
| `Keyword` | `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` |
| `RelativePath` | POSIX path; non-empty; not absolute; no backslash, NUL, empty segment, `.` or `..`; each segment matches `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` |
| `RelativeFile` | `RelativePath` resolving to one regular non-symlink file |
| `RelativeDir` | `RelativePath` resolving to one non-symlink directory |
| `NonBlankText` | String whose outer-trimmed value is non-empty |
| `EnvironmentName` | `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$` |

YAML booleans never satisfy integer fields. Null is never accepted; optional means absent.

## 2. Top-level field table

Every object uses `additionalProperties: false`, except the two explicitly scoped frozen-JSON maps noted below.

| Field | Required | Type/default | Semantics |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `schema_version` | yes | integer, exactly `2` | No numeric string or float |
| `id` | yes | `PackId` | Must exactly equal the owner directory name |
| `name` | yes | `NonBlankText` | Display name |
| `version` | yes | `ReleaseVersion` | Pack release only; never a migration version |
| `description` | no | text, default `""` | Descriptive only |
| `status` | no | `active\|experimental\|deprecated`, default `active` | `stub` is deleted |
| `visibility` | no | `visible\|hidden`, default `visible` | Discovery display behavior |
| `domain` | no | `general\|development\|editorial\|generation\|infrastructure\|integration\|media\|system`, default `general` | Descriptive taxonomy |
| `stability` | no | `stable\|experimental\|deprecated`, default `stable` | Descriptive taxonomy |
| `support` | no | `project\|core\|community`, default `project` | Descriptive taxonomy |
| `keywords` | no | unique `Keyword[]`, default `[]` | Search terms |
| `capabilities` | no | unique `LowerIdent[]`, default `[]` | Search/explanation tags only; never registry authority |
| `permissions` | no | `Permission[]`, default `[]` | Existing disclosure/eligibility mechanism |
| `content` | no | `ContentRoots`, default `{}` | Typed component/content roots |
| `extensions` | no | `Extensions`, default `{}` | Existing typed extension declarations |
| `aliases` | no | `Alias[]`, default `[]` | Live typed aliases only |
| `agent` | no | `AgentRouting`, default empty values | Routing prose; not capability authority |
| `documentation` | no | tagged `Documentation` object | Required by bundled admission unless explicit internal opt-out |
| `secrets` | no | `Secret[]`, default `[]` | Existing install/trust disclosure |
| `dependencies` | no | `RuntimeDependencies`, default empty lists | Python/npm/system runtime disclosure; unrelated to database dependencies |
| `astrid_version` | no | `ReleaseVersion` | Minimum compatible Astrid release metadata; not trust |
| `database` | no | `DatabaseContribution` | Bundled-only SQLite contribution |
| `resources` | no | `SupplementalResource[]`, default `[]` | Runtime/doc assets not reached by another typed declaration |
| `authoring_only` | no | `AuthoringExclusion[]`, default `[]` | Explicit non-wheel classification |

At least one of `content`, `extensions`, `documentation`, `database`, or `resources` must contribute a non-empty value.

The following v1 fields are prohibited: `origin`, `install_tier`, `pack_type`, `metadata`, and `docs`. Provenance replaces the first three; `documentation` replaces `docs`; arbitrary top-level metadata is not retained.

## 3. Nested shapes

### `content`

All keys are optional `RelativeDir` values:

```yaml
content:
  executors: executors
  orchestrators: orchestrators
  elements: elements
  schemas: schemas
  examples: examples
  docs: docs
```

There is no fallback tree scan when a v2 content root is absent. Executor, orchestrator, and element roots use the existing typed component loaders. Schema/example/docs directories expand recursively as declared resources.

### `permissions`

```yaml
permissions:
  - id: subprocess
    reason: Runs ffmpeg.
    access: optional nonblank text
    services: [optional-service-name]
```

- `id` enum: `project_files`, `network`, `subprocess`, `environment`, `accelerator`, `external_services`.
- Exact keys: `id`, `reason`, optional `access`, optional `services`.
- Duplicate permission IDs reject.
- `reason` is required.
- Service names are trimmed, duplicate-rejected, and canonically sorted.

### `aliases`

```yaml
aliases:
  - kind: executor
    alias: rendering.render_video
    canonical_id: rendering.render
```

- Exact keys: `kind`, `alias`, `canonical_id`.
- `kind` enum: `executor`, `orchestrator`, `renderer`, `planner`, `finalizer`.
- Both IDs are `QualifiedId`, distinct, and owned by the declaring pack.
- Duplicate `(kind, alias)` rejects.
- `deprecated` and `deprecation_message` are prohibited.
- Every current deprecated alias is deleted, including the 49 `builtin.*` aliases and any remaining `external.*`/legacy aliases. V2 aliases cannot be compatibility shims.

### `agent`

```yaml
agent:
  purpose: optional prose
  do_not_use_for: optional prose
  normal_entrypoints: [optional trimmed routing strings]
  required_context: [optional/owner-relative.md]
```

Only these four keys exist. `required_context` entries are `RelativeFile` resources and enter the resource closure. Routing strings are descriptive; the typed registries remain authoritative.

### `documentation`

Exactly one tagged shape:

```yaml
documentation:
  kind: skill
  path: skill/SKILL.md
```

```yaml
documentation:
  kind: agents
  path: AGENTS.md
```

```yaml
documentation:
  kind: none
  reason: Non-user-facing internal utility pack.
```

Rules:

- `skill` requires the exact path `skill/SKILL.md`.
- `agents` requires the exact path `AGENTS.md`.
- `none` permits only `kind` and a nonblank `reason`.
- Bundled admission rejects `none` unless the coverage ledger classifies the pack as non-user-facing internal utility.
- For this run, all 22 retained product packs are user/agent-facing and therefore use `skill` or `agents`; there are no product-pack opt-outs.

### `secrets`

```yaml
secrets:
  - name: FAL_KEY
    required: true
    description: optional prose
```

Exact keys are `name`, optional `required` defaulting to `false`, and optional `description`. Names are unique `EnvironmentName` values.

### `dependencies`

```yaml
dependencies:
  python: ["package>=1"]
  npm: ["package@1"]
  system: ["ffmpeg"]
```

Only `python`, `npm`, and `system` exist. Each is a duplicate-free list of trimmed nonblank strings. These remain install/trust disclosures and do not participate in pack or migration solving.

### `resources`

```yaml
resources:
  - path: server/blender-render-api.service
    kind: service
```

Exact keys are `path` and `kind`. `kind` enum:

- `runtime`
- `schema`
- `template`
- `documentation`
- `service`
- `data`

A path may name a file or directory. Directories expand recursively. A physical file may be reached by only one typed or supplemental declaration.

### `authoring_only`

```yaml
authoring_only:
  - path: executors/generate_image/golden
    kind: golden
    reason: Provider demonstration inputs; not required at runtime.
```

Exact keys are `path`, `kind`, and `reason`. `kind` enum:

- `test`
- `golden`
- `development_fixture`
- `authoring_document`
- `build_output`
- `placeholder`

These paths must exist in source, cannot overlap declared resources, and must not occur in the wheel.

### `extensions`

The v1 extension families remain, but string/object dual forms are removed and all nested objects are strict:

```yaml
extensions:
  generation:
    backends:
      - id: backend_id
        label: optional
        module: python.module
        class: ClassName
        init_kwargs: {}        # scoped arbitrary frozen JSON
    features:
      - id: feature_id
        label: optional
        description: optional
    modes:
      - id: mode_id
        label: optional
        description: optional

  elements:
    kinds:
      - id: effects
        singular: optional
        plural: optional
        label: optional
        description: optional

  timeline:
    kinds:
      - catalog: transition  # transition|clip|track
        id: cross_fade
        aliases: []
        default: false

  rendering:
    renderers: [backends/remotion/renderer.yaml]
    planners: [planners/layer_stack/planner.yaml]
    finalizers: [finalizers/ffmpeg/finalizer.yaml]

  schemas: {}                # explicitly scoped arbitrary frozen JSON

  artifact_types:
    types:
      - id: artifact_id
        aliases: []
        description: optional
```

All IDs and paths are duplicate-free. Rendering manifest paths are `RelativeFile` resources and are traversed through the existing typed renderer/planner/finalizer validators.

### `database`

```yaml
database:
  depends_on:
    - pack: core
      min_migration: 1
  migrations:
    - version: 1
      name: initial
      path: migrations/0001_initial.sql
      tables: [timelines]
  stream_types: [timeline.timeline]
  event_kinds: [timeline.created]
  command_kinds: [timeline.create]
  repositories: [TimelineRepository]
  conformance: [replay]
  cli_mounts:
    timelines: timelines
  bridge_mounts: [timelines]
```

All nine fields are required, even when empty:

| Field | Exact type |
|---|---|
| `depends_on` | Unique `{pack: PackId, min_migration: positive integer}[]` |
| `migrations` | Non-empty `Migration[]` |
| `stream_types` | Unique `DottedVocabulary[]` |
| `event_kinds` | Unique `DottedVocabulary[]` |
| `command_kinds` | Unique `DottedVocabulary[]` |
| `repositories` | Unique `RepositoryId[]` |
| `conformance` | Unique `LowerIdent[]` |
| `cli_mounts` | Map `LowerIdent →` one-or-more single-space-separated `LowerIdent` tokens |
| `bridge_mounts` | Unique `LowerIdent[]` |

Each migration contains exactly:

- `version`: positive integer; unique within the pack.
- `name`: `MigrationName`; unique within the pack.
- `path`: `RelativeFile` ending in `.sql`; unique within the pack.
- `tables`: non-empty, unique `LowerIdent[]`; a table may appear in only one migration in the pack and only one database contribution globally.

Migration descriptors must already appear in strictly increasing version order. YAML never contains columns, indexes, constraints, SQL transformations, applied state, checksums, or a mutable head.

## 4. Normalization and immutable model

One load produces a manifest-derived `PackDefinition` containing no root, source, trust, install state, or manifest path.

Normalization is exact:

- Outer whitespace is stripped from prose and disclosure strings.
- Identifiers must already have canonical case; the loader never lowercases them.
- Paths are converted to canonical POSIX relative strings and then to confined resource handles.
- Ordered operational lists preserve declared order only for `agent.normal_entrypoints`.
- Migrations must be declared in increasing version order and retain that order.
- Set-like lists—keywords, capability tags, permissions, services, aliases, dependencies, vocabulary, repositories, conformance, resources, and exclusions—reject duplicates and normalize to deterministic lexical order.
- Mappings normalize by sorted key.
- Arbitrary JSON is permitted only in `extensions.generation.backends[].init_kwargs` and `extensions.schemas`; it is recursively frozen into immutable mappings/tuples.
- No raw manifest mapping survives in the public canonical object.
- `PackDefinition`, all nested declarations, `CatalogEntry`, provenance, resource handles, database projections, and snapshots are frozen/slot-based immutable values.
- Serialization emits canonical keys and lists from normalized values; it never emits loader roots or trust as manifest data.

The catalog envelope owns:

```text
CatalogEntry
├── definition: PackDefinition
├── provenance: CatalogProvenance
├── manifest: ResourceHandle
└── resources: tuple[ResourceHandle, ...]
```

This directly removes the current `PackDefinition.root`, `manifest_path`, self-asserted `origin`, `install_tier`, and `pack_type` mixture.

## 5. Golden and invalid fixtures

Required golden fixtures:

1. Capability-only: executor content, permission, documentation, no database.
2. Database-only: complete database block and documentation, no capability roots.
3. Combined: references-style content/database/SDK/CLI/documentation declaration.

Required invalid fixtures include:

- Missing, string, float, boolean, or non-2 `schema_version`.
- `pack.yml`, `pack.json`, schema-less YAML, flat parser input, arbitrary manifest path, and `schema-pack.yaml`.
- Folder/ID mismatch and bad release version.
- Every unknown top-level and nested field class.
- Every prohibited v1 field.
- Null optional values.
- Duplicate set-like entries.
- Alias ownership violations or deprecated alias fields.
- Malformed permissions and documentation unions.
- Absolute, backslash, empty, traversal, symlink, missing, wrong-kind, or duplicate resource paths.
- Resource/authoring overlap.
- External `database`.
- Missing dependency, dependency on capability-only pack, bad minimum head, self-dependency, cycle, and duplicate dependency.
- Unordered/duplicate migrations, duplicate tables, non-SQL path.
- Provenance spoofing attempts.
- Undeclared bundled non-Python files and wheel-only/source-only resources.

---

# II. Catalog construction, precedence, trust, and lifetime

## 1. Bundled catalog

`BundledCatalog` is built from the installed/source `astrid/packs` package root:

1. Enumerate direct child directories in lexical order.
2. Ignore `_core`, cache directories, and directories without `pack.yaml`.
3. Require exactly one `pack.yaml`; alternate pack filenames are errors.
4. Load, normalize, validate, resolve, classify, and digest each retained bundled pack.
5. Reject duplicate bundled IDs fatally.
6. Cache the resulting immutable catalog once per process.

The empty `builtin` directory is removed, not ignored or represented by a tombstone at runtime.

The bundled catalog cache contains declarations and resources only. It never contains a database connection, service, repository, CLI parser, bridge, executor instance, or application object.

## 2. Dynamic candidate capture

Each top-level operation captures local/external candidates once from the current layers:

1. `local`
2. explicit extra roots, in caller-given root order
3. `ASTRID_PACKS_PATH`, in environment path order
4. installed active revisions, sorted by pack ID

Within a root, candidates are sorted by pack ID and canonical manifest path. Repeated references to the same resolved root and manifest digest are deduplicated.

Read-only discovery never creates `astrid/packs/local`; explicit create/fork operations own that mutation.

## 3. `CatalogSnapshot`

Every top-level operation constructs exactly one immutable snapshot:

```text
CatalogSnapshot
├── bundled_catalog
├── entries_by_id
├── ordered_entries
├── rejected_candidates
├── snapshot_fingerprint
└── construction_context
```

Construction combines the cached bundled catalog with that operation’s dynamic capture using precedence:

```text
bundled > local > explicit-extra > environment > installed
```

Duplicate policy:

- Bundled IDs are reserved and always win.
- An identical resolved root/digest encountered twice is deduplicated.
- Any other later candidate with an already-winning ID is excluded and recorded in `rejected_candidates` as `duplicate_id`, including winner and loser provenance.
- External discovery may continue after an invalid candidate, preserving current fault isolation.
- Direct validation/install of the rejected candidate returns a hard error.
- Lower-precedence candidates never partially contribute aliases, capabilities, documentation, or resources after losing pack identity.

Trust/admission policy:

| Source | Inspect | Capability execution | Database |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| bundled | yes | yes | yes |
| local | yes | yes | no |
| explicit extra | yes | yes | no |
| environment | yes | no | no |
| installed | yes | only active validated revision with accepted permissions | no |

A manifest’s descriptive fields cannot change this table.

When any non-bundled candidate contains `database`, the entire candidate is rejected as `external_database_forbidden` immediately after structural normalization and provenance assignment—before resource resolution, migration SQL reads, alias registration, or typed-registry projection.

## 4. Operation/lifetime invariant

A single snapshot is threaded through every projection used by one operation:

- One CLI invocation.
- One `sdk.discover`, `get_capability`, or `invoke` call.
- One `AstridClient.open()` lifetime.
- One standard application composition/lifetime.
- One serve composition/lifetime.
- One doctor run.
- One inspect command.
- One backup create or restore validation.
- One install validation/admission.
- One package/source audit.

Consumers may not rescan or reconstruct a snapshot internally. Convenience entry points may create a snapshot only when they are themselves the top-level boundary.

An `AstridClient` or standard application holds its snapshot until `close()`. Filesystem/install changes become visible only to the next operation/application.

Every resource handle records its owner-relative path, resolved root, file kind, size, and SHA-256 at snapshot construction. Reads verify the recorded digest. If an external revision changes during the operation, the handle raises `CatalogSnapshotStaleError`; the consumer never reparses or silently adopts changed bytes.

Existing registries accept `CatalogSnapshot` or the narrow projection derived from it. No registry, SDK service, renderer loader, skill index, doctor, backup, or inspect path calls discovery independently.

---

# III. Bounded bundled-resource classification and audit

The resource gate is deliberately closed-world and does not attempt perfect static analysis.

## 1. Inventory boundary

For each bundled pack root:

1. Walk deterministically without following symlinks.
2. Reject every symlink encountered.
3. Inventory every regular file except `.py` source files and cache/build metadata already prohibited by layout validation.
4. Treat `pack.yaml` as the implicit canonical-manifest resource.
5. Require every other inventoried file to have exactly one classification:

   - reached through a typed declaration;
   - reached through one supplemental `resources` entry; or
   - covered by one `authoring_only` entry.

Zero classifications is `undeclared_file`. More than one is `duplicate_classification`.

## 2. Typed reachability

Typed seeds and edges are exact:

- `content.executors`, `orchestrators`, and `elements` locate component roots through existing typed component validators; they do not declare every file below those roots wholesale.
- Component manifests, their schema-declared path fields, their documented `STAGE.md` convention, declared assets, and declared schema/doc routes become resource edges.
- `content.schemas`, `content.examples`, and `content.docs` recursively classify their directories.
- Rendering extension lists classify their renderer/planner/finalizer manifests; existing typed validators enumerate their path-valued fields.
- `database.migrations[].path` classifies SQL.
- `documentation.path` and `agent.required_context` classify documentation.
- `resources[].path` classifies a supplemental file or recursively expands a directory.
- Nested component `skill/SKILL.md`, requirements files, templates, service units, and other opaque assets must be reached by a typed component declaration or listed explicitly under `resources`.

Directories expand in lexical relative-path order. Empty declared directories reject.

## 3. Targeted runtime-use AST check

Scan only runtime `.py` files under bundled pack roots, excluding paths classified as test, golden, development fixture, or build output.

The checker recognizes:

- `open(...)`
- `Path.open`, `read_text`, `read_bytes`
- `importlib.resources.files`, `open_text`, `open_binary`
- `pkgutil.get_data`
- simple `Path(__file__)`/`__file__`-derived constants and `/`, `joinpath`, `with_name`, or `parent` literal path composition
- the existing executor-folder convention that probes `requirements*.txt`
- the Blender deployment service-unit lookup
- existing typed component/rendering manifest resource APIs

It performs only intra-module constant propagation for simple literal assignments. Arbitrary user-supplied paths, generated outputs, network inputs, and dynamic project paths are not classified as pack resources.

Any statically resolvable pack-relative read must resolve to the declared closure. A statically resolvable read outside the pack root must either:

- move the asset into its owning pack; or
- resolve through an explicitly kernel-owned resource recorded in the coverage ledger.

It cannot be papered over with an authoring exclusion.

Known runtime filenames—migration SQL, component manifests, `STAGE.md`, `SKILL.md`, `requirements*.txt`, referenced schemas/templates, and `.service` files—cannot use an authoring-only classification.

## 4. Source/wheel equality

The source audit emits the declared closure as sorted records:

```json
{
  "pack_id": "blender",
  "path": "server/blender-render-api.service",
  "kind": "service",
  "size": 1234,
  "sha256": "..."
}
```

The clean-wheel audit reconstructs the bundled catalog in a source-isolated process and emits the same records.

Required equality:

- Same pack IDs.
- Same normalized manifest definitions.
- Same relative resource paths.
- Same resource kinds, sizes, and SHA-256 values.
- Same database declarations and derived heads.
- Same documentation routes.
- Same bundled provenance class.

Absolute roots are intentionally different and are not compared.

The reverse audit also fails if the wheel contains an undeclared bundled non-Python file or any `authoring_only` file.

Use ordinary setuptools package-data configuration; do not add a packaging backend.

---

# IV. Concrete audit-only coverage ledger

## 1. Files and commands

Committed reviewed ledger:

```text
docs/contracts/canonical-pack-coverage.json
```

Implementation:

```text
scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py
```

Boundary commands:

```bash
python3 scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py inventory \
  --root . \
  --output .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/inventory.json

python3 scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py check \
  --inventory .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/inventory.json \
  --ledger docs/contracts/canonical-pack-coverage.json \
  --output .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/check.json

python3 scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py diff \
  --before .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<previous>/inventory.json \
  --after .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/inventory.json \
  --output .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/diff.json
```

`check` exits nonzero for any issue. Runtime code never imports the script, ledger, inventory, or diff.

## 2. Canonical inventory inputs

The inventory command reads only:

- The canonical bundled `CatalogSnapshot`.
- Typed executor, orchestrator, element, renderer, planner, and finalizer projections.
- The canonical database projection.
- The resource-closure result.
- Structured documentation routes.
- Code-declared kernel migration/vocabulary/repository constants.
- Exact AST symbol inspection of the existing application composition, SDK service fields, product/operational CLI declarations, bridge mounts, doctor, backup, restore, inspect, package audit, and agent-census generator.

It does not use the reviewed ledger to discover surfaces and does not broadly grep arbitrary text.

## 3. Inventory surface IDs

Exact forms:

```text
pack:<pack_id>
executor:<qualified_id>
orchestrator:<qualified_id>
element:<kind>/<id>
renderer:<qualified_id>
planner:<qualified_id>
finalizer:<qualified_id>
alias:<kind>:<qualified_id>
migration:<pack_id>/<integer>
table:<table>
stream_type:<dotted_name>
event_kind:<dotted_name>
command_kind:<dotted_name>
repository:<RepositoryId>
conformance:<pack_id>/<dimension>
cli_mount:<space-separated path>
sdk_surface:<stable service or method id>
bridge_mount:<token>
runtime_resource:<pack_id>/<relative path>
agent_document:<pack_id>/<relative path>
operational_consumer:<application|doctor|backup|restore|inspect|package|agent_census>
```

Rows are sorted by `(kind, surface_id)`. Evidence paths and consumers are unique and sorted.

## 4. Ledger schema

```json
{
  "schema": "astrid.canonical_pack_coverage.v1",
  "surfaces": [
    {
      "surface_id": "table:timelines",
      "kind": "table",
      "disposition": "active",
      "owner": "pack:timeline",
      "evidence_paths": [
        "astrid/packs/timeline/pack.yaml",
        "astrid/packs/timeline/migrations/0001_initial.sql"
      ],
      "projection_consumers": [
        "operational_consumer:application",
        "operational_consumer:doctor"
      ]
    },
    {
      "surface_id": "alias:executor:builtin.render",
      "kind": "alias",
      "disposition": "deleted",
      "owner": null,
      "justification": "Deprecated compatibility alias removed by the beta hard cut.",
      "evidence_paths": [
        "baseline:7ac50c12e8e4d90988fee603ffdb9896e5628792:astrid/packs/rendering/pack.yaml"
      ],
      "projection_consumers": []
    }
  ]
}
```

Rules:

- `disposition` is `active` or `deleted`.
- Active owner is exactly `pack:<PackId>` or `kernel:<LowerIdent>`.
- Deleted rows have `owner: null`, a nonblank justification, immutable baseline evidence, and no consumers.
- Pack-owned rows must agree with the owner derived from their canonical catalog/database/resource projection. A manual override is impossible.
- Kernel rows require a nonblank justification, at least one implementation evidence path, at least one test/contract evidence path, and must not be representable by an existing pack declaration.
- Every active row has at least one resolvable evidence path and one valid consumer unless its kind is itself `operational_consumer`.
- Every consumer reference must resolve to another active inventory row.
- `builtin` and every deprecated alias are deletion records, never synthetic pack or kernel ownership.
- The 49 known `builtin.*` aliases are an exact baseline assertion; WP0 additionally records and deletes every other deprecated alias discovered at the pinned source.

## 5. Exact comparisons

`check` emits and fails on:

- `missing`: inventory surface has no ledger row.
- `stale`: active ledger row is absent from inventory or has missing evidence.
- `duplicate`: repeated surface ID or repeated classification.
- `conflict`: ledger owner differs from the mechanically derived owner, or inventory derives multiple owners.
- `deleted_still_active`: a deleted ledger ID is present in inventory.
- `invalid_kernel_justification`: missing implementation/test evidence or pack-representable ownership.
- `invalid_consumer`: missing, deleted, or cyclic self-only consumer reference.
- `unclassified`: inventory row has no valid active owner.
- `undeclared_resource`: resource audit failure.
- `unexpected_owner`: pack referenced by owner is absent from the bundled catalog.

Boundary diff records exact added, removed, changed-owner, changed-evidence, changed-consumer, and changed-resource-digest sets. Reviewer approval is required for every non-empty category.

WP0’s immutable baseline inventory is evidence only. It is never imported by final code or treated as current truth.

---

# V. Deterministic database composition and migration semantics

## 1. Projection nodes

The database graph contains:

- One synthetic code-declared `core` node, head `1`, using existing core SQL and vocabulary.
- Every `CatalogSnapshot` entry whose provenance is bundled and whose definition contains `database`.

No pack ID allowlist or standard tuple participates.

Every selected node contains:

- ID.
- Immutable database declaration.
- Owner resource handles.
- Derived migration head.
- Provenance.
- Existing collision projection.

A synthetic trusted bundled database pack added to a test snapshot must enter composition without changing composition code.

## 2. Migration head and dependency meaning

For node `P`:

```text
head(P) = max(P.migrations[].version)
```

`depends_on: {pack: Q, min_migration: N}` means:

- `Q` must be a selected database node or `core`.
- `head(Q) >= N`.
- All migrations of `Q` precede every migration of `P`.
- It does not compare pack release versions.
- It does not solve ranges.
- It does not inspect project-applied state during graph construction.

Validation before opening a database:

- Every database pack has a direct or transitive dependency path to `core`.
- No dependency targets the declaring pack.
- No duplicate target.
- No target with no database migrations.
- Every minimum is a positive integer no greater than the target head.
- The graph is acyclic.
- All current table, migration identity/name, vocabulary, repository, CLI, and bridge collision classes remain rejected before database open.

## 3. Exact standard graph

After conversion:

```text
core@1
├── references@1  requires core >= 1
├── runaway@1     requires core >= 1
├── shots@1       requires core >= 1
└── timeline@1    requires core >= 1
```

The pack release versions remain strings such as `1.0.0`; they do not enter this graph.

## 4. Ordering and tie-break

Use deterministic Kahn topological sorting:

1. Create an edge `dependency → dependent`.
2. Seed a min-heap with every zero-indegree node.
3. Heap key is `(0 if id == "core" else 1, pack_id)`.
4. Pop the lowest key, append it, decrement dependents, and push newly ready nodes using the same key.
5. If fewer nodes are emitted than selected, return the lexical cycle path as an error.
6. Within a node, emit migrations by increasing integer version.

The current graph therefore orders:

```text
core/1
references/1
runaway/1
shots/1
timeline/1
```

Before runaway is present, the retained order is:

```text
core/1
references/1
shots/1
timeline/1
```

On a current three-pack database, those existing keys are skipped and only `runaway/1` is applied. Existing `schema_migrations` rows are not rewritten or resequenced.

## 5. Preserved migration identity and safety

For all current migrations preserve exactly:

- `pack`.
- Integer `version`.
- `name` (`initial`).
- SQL bytes.
- Owned tables.
- SHA-256 computation.
- Name/checksum drift checks.
- Too-new and unknown-pack refusal.
- Per-migration `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.
- DDL/DML and migration-row atomicity.
- Read-only compatibility probing.
- `schema_migrations` as the sole applied-state record.

Replace `pack_resource_root(pack_id)` with the registered owner `ResourceHandle`; the runner never reconstructs `astrid/packs/<id>`.

Core remains explicit code-owned kernel behavior.

---

# VI. Documentation, census, and per-pack preservation

- All 22 retained product packs declare direct structured docs.
- Add direct skills for blender, timeline, shots, references, and runaway.
- Repair media’s frontmatter.
- Package all declared pack and nested component skills.
- `_core` remains a skill-only kernel shell with no `pack.yaml`.
- Extend the existing generator so one snapshot produces both the capability index and canonical pack census.
- Generated census fields: ID, name, contribution categories, capability counts, database tables/head, documentation route, and ownership link.
- The census includes neither DDL nor applied database state.
- Check mode fails on drift.
- Delete independent pack-skill inventory and raw directory reparsing; skills consume snapshot documentation/component projections.

Maintain the four-pack preservation matrix for timeline, shots, references, and runaway across:

- migration;
- repository;
- events;
- commands;
- CLI;
- SDK;
- bridge;
- conformance.

Every cell has baseline and final evidence or an evidence-backed `N/A`. Broad suite success cannot replace the matrix.

# VII. Work packages

## WP0 — immutable baseline and ownership reconciliation

Estimate: **0.45–0.65 week**. No dependencies.

- Verify custody source `7ac50c12e8e4d90988fee603ffdb9896e5628792`.
- Capture immutable counts and inventories: 19 capability manifests, four schema manifests, `_core`, 64 executors, 12 orchestrators, 10 elements, eight rendering extensions, four database packs, and 18 existing skills.
- Record the 23 current product directories, deletion of `builtin`, and final 22-pack expectation.
- Capture every active customization surface and initial owner classification.
- Record the 49 `builtin.*` aliases and all other deprecated aliases as intended deletions.
- Capture current database ordering, migration identities/checksums, application/open behavior, repositories, vocabulary, CLI/SDK/bridge behavior, doctor, backup, restore, and installed-wheel behavior.
- Establish the four-pack behavior matrix and 15-row completion matrix.
- Record exact exclusions for `.oracle`, planning artifacts, tests, golden authoring inputs, build outputs, caches, unrelated training manifests, and non-pack component formats.
- Do not build a wheel or run the full suite.
- Treat the runaway demo input as a temporary deterministic fixture.

Exit: every baseline surface is classified as pack, kernel, or intended deletion; no research question remains.

- Land immutable normalized types.
- Land the three golden fixtures and all invalid fixture classes.
- Land explicit provenance/resource types.
- Pass schema and normalization tests.
- Obtain cumulative review before any bundled manifest conversion.

Then:

- Evolve `PackDefinition` rather than add a parallel product model.
- Implement the only v2 `pack.yaml` loader.
- Delete fallback parsing from the new path.
- Implement confined resource handles and digest-verifying reads.
- Implement process-cached `BundledCatalog`.
- Implement dynamic candidate capture and `CatalogSnapshot`.
- Implement exact precedence, duplicate diagnostics, trust, and external-database rejection.
- Change local pack creation to emit strict capability-only v2.
- Implement the bounded resource classifier and targeted AST audit.
- Implement `scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py` and its schemas.
- Adapt typed registry constructors to accept snapshot projections without changing service construction ownership.

Exit: schema, loader, normalized definition, provenance, resource resolver, snapshot semantics, resource audit, and coverage tooling are executable and fixture-proven.

- Run focused package-local ledger/resource/matrix checks without retaining extra immutable artifacts.

```

# VIII. Cumulative review cadence

1. **Contract-freeze subgate:** during WP1, before bundled conversion.
2. **Boundary A:** after WP2—v2 catalog, all bundled declarations, docs, and resource closure.
3. **Boundary B:** after WP3—catalog-derived database composition and migration hard cut.
4. **Boundary C:** after WP5—all consumers, deletions, audits, and source packaging configuration.
5. **Final:** after WP6—single-wheel and complete evidence closure.

At A, B, C, and final:

- Generate one immutable coverage inventory/check/diff artifact set.
- Regenerate relevant matrices.
- Converge code, receipts, artifacts, and status on one checkpoint.
- Run one cumulative independent review pass by default.
- Resolve accepted findings and obtain a fresh pass before continuing.

Package-local checks may run during implementation, but immutable cumulative artifacts are produced only at A, B, C, and final.

---

# IX. Done-criterion traceability

| Frozen criterion | Primary proof |
|---|---|
| 1. Every bundled product pack strict v2 | WP1–WP2, A, WP5–WP6 |
| 2. No independent identity parsing | Snapshot invariant, WP4–WP6 |
| 3. Zero-unclassified ledger | WP0–WP2, A/B/C, final |
| 4. Manifest-derived database composition | WP3 synthetic-pack test, B, final |
| 5. Four-pack behavior preserved | WP0 matrix, WP2–WP4, B/C/final |
| 6. Owner-relative migrations and safety | Exact database contract, WP3, final SQLite matrix |
| 7. Operational agreement | Snapshot threading, WP4, C/final |
| 8. Structured docs and `_core` census | WP2, WP4–WP6 |
| 9. Inspect and doctor | WP4, C/final |
| 10. Clean-wheel closure | Resource algorithm, WP5, single wheel in WP6 |
| 11. Legacy deletion | WP5 exact paths/imports/AST gates |

| 13. Three golden forms | WP1 contract gate and final |
| 14. Focused/full validation or baseline proof | WP0 and single authoritative WP6 flow |
| 15. Evidence matrix and oracle | WP0 seed, WP6 completion |

# X. Explicit anti-pattern rejections

- No hidden schema-pack subsystem. Only reusable migration/collision algorithms survive.
- No fixed composition disguise. Named packs appear only in expected fixtures and preservation evidence.
- No universal service locator. The catalog contains declarations; typed registries and static factories construct runtime objects.
- No runtime coverage ledger. It is audit-only.
- No YAML DDL or applied state.
- No manifest-supplied trust.
- No operation-internal rescanning.
- No lower-precedence partial contribution after duplicate-ID loss.
- No perfect/general static analysis. Resource proof is a bounded closed-world classification plus targeted known-API AST checks.
- No arbitrary authoring exclusion for known runtime assets.
- No dependency solver, release-version solving, project lock, enable/disable/purge, database uninstall, marketplace, signing, sandboxing, or UI.
- No external SQL.
- No dynamically unloadable kernel.
- No v1, alternate filenames, schema-less manifests, dual reads, compatibility exports, deprecated aliases, or fallbacks.
- No generalized repository/service/CLI/bridge factory framework.
- No custom packaging backend.
- No broad repository-wide regex policy scanner.
- No repeated wheel builds.
- No repeated immutable ledger/matrix artifacts between cumulative boundaries.
- No scope expansion into models, LoRAs, taxonomies, or unrelated kernel primitives.
- No success while an active bypass, unclassified surface, missing document, undeclared resource, source/wheel mismatch, or independent consumer rescan remains.

## Final disposition

This replacement closes every Wave 2 reopening with an executable boundary:

- v2 has an exact field grammar and normalization contract;
- one immutable `CatalogSnapshot` governs each operation;
- precedence, duplicates, trust, staleness, and external database rejection are deterministic;
- bundled resources have a bounded complete classification algorithm;
- the coverage ledger has concrete commands, schema, inputs, and diff semantics;
- migration dependencies compare exact integer migration heads with a defined graph and tie-break;
- installed proof uses one wheel built once;
- cumulative artifact ceremony is limited to A, B, C, and final.

The next Phase 3 action is an exact Sol `STABLE` check against this complete snapshot. If the response is exactly `STABLE`, run a fresh full settled-plan wave before accepting the plan as settled.

# Phase 3 complete replacement plan — canonical pack beta hard cut

Status: **Wave 2 revised replacement; ready for an exact `STABLE` check and then a fresh settled-plan wave**.

This plan supersedes the current plan in full. It incorporates every accepted Wave 1 and Wave 2 synthesis item while preserving the complete North Star, frozen goal, authorization boundaries, done criteria, validation contract, and anti-pattern rejections.

## Determination

- Estimated implementation effort: **6.2–8.6 engineer-weeks**, including cumulative reviews and expected rework.
- Huge run: **yes**.
- Difficulty: **5/5**.
- Proposed `[XHARD]` tasks: **none**. The implementation remains decomposable normal Luna work; the Phase 4 oracle makes the final classification.
- New research or exploration lanes: **none**.
- Open planning questions: **none**.
- Execution order: immutable baseline → executable v2 contract/catalog → bundled conversion and resource closure → database hard cut → operational convergence → deletion/audit/packaging → one-build validation and evidence closure.
- The estimate increases from 5.7–8.0 weeks because Wave 2 makes three previously implicit systems concrete: operation-scoped snapshots, the bounded resource classifier, and the reproducible coverage generator. No product scope is added.
- The missing runaway demo input remains a deterministic temporary-project fixture repair unless custody evidence proves it is an intentional packaged asset.

## Mandate and boundaries

Implement one strict canonical pack system:

- Every retained bundled product pack is declared by exactly one `pack.yaml` with integer `schema_version: 2`.
- The empty `builtin` product pack is deleted, leaving **22 retained product packs**, plus irreducible `_core` guidance.
- `timeline`, `shots`, `references`, and `runaway` become ordinary packs with `database` contributions.
- Existing typed registries, migration safety, repositories, `DatabaseWriter`, `UnitOfWork`, static application/service/CLI/bridge factories, SDK behavior, and conformance semantics remain.
- Bundled trust comes exclusively from loader provenance.
- External packs remain capability-only. An external manifest containing `database` is rejected as a whole before resolving its declared resources or reading SQL.
- Every bundled customization is owned by a pack or explicitly classified as irreducible kernel behavior.
- Every user/agent-facing bundled pack has packaged structured documentation.
- No per-project composition state, dependency solver, dynamic database plugin system, marketplace, lifecycle machinery, or generalized service container is added.

The North Star guides implementation but cannot widen the frozen goal.

# I. Exact v2 manifest contract

WP1 must land this contract as JSON Schema, normalized immutable Python types, golden fixtures, and invalid fixtures before bundled conversion begins.

## 1. Lexical types

| Type | Exact rule |
|---|---|
| `PackId` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` |
| `ReleaseVersion` | `^(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)\.(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)\.(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)$`; no prerelease/build suffix |
| `LowerIdent` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` |
| `QualifiedId` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` |
| `DottedVocabulary` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*)+$` |
| `RepositoryId` | `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$` |
| `MigrationName` | `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` |
| `Keyword` | `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` |
| `RelativePath` | POSIX path; non-empty; not absolute; no backslash, NUL, empty segment, `.` or `..`; each segment matches `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` |
| `RelativeFile` | `RelativePath` resolving to one regular non-symlink file |
| `RelativeDir` | `RelativePath` resolving to one non-symlink directory |
| `NonBlankText` | String whose outer-trimmed value is non-empty |
| `EnvironmentName` | `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$` |

YAML booleans never satisfy integer fields. Null is never accepted; optional means absent.

## 2. Top-level field table

Every object uses `additionalProperties: false`, except the two explicitly scoped frozen-JSON maps noted below.

| Field | Required | Type/default | Semantics |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `schema_version` | yes | integer, exactly `2` | No numeric string or float |
| `id` | yes | `PackId` | Must exactly equal the owner directory name |
| `name` | yes | `NonBlankText` | Display name |
| `version` | yes | `ReleaseVersion` | Pack release only; never a migration version |
| `description` | no | text, default `""` | Descriptive only |
| `status` | no | `active\|experimental\|deprecated`, default `active` | `stub` is deleted |
| `visibility` | no | `visible\|hidden`, default `visible` | Discovery display behavior |
| `domain` | no | `general\|development\|editorial\|generation\|infrastructure\|integration\|media\|system`, default `general` | Descriptive taxonomy |
| `stability` | no | `stable\|experimental\|deprecated`, default `stable` | Descriptive taxonomy |
| `support` | no | `project\|core\|community`, default `project` | Descriptive taxonomy |
| `keywords` | no | unique `Keyword[]`, default `[]` | Search terms |
| `capabilities` | no | unique `LowerIdent[]`, default `[]` | Search/explanation tags only; never registry authority |
| `permissions` | no | `Permission[]`, default `[]` | Existing disclosure/eligibility mechanism |
| `content` | no | `ContentRoots`, default `{}` | Typed component/content roots |
| `extensions` | no | `Extensions`, default `{}` | Existing typed extension declarations |
| `aliases` | no | `Alias[]`, default `[]` | Live typed aliases only |
| `agent` | no | `AgentRouting`, default empty values | Routing prose; not capability authority |
| `documentation` | no | tagged `Documentation` object | Required by bundled admission unless explicit internal opt-out |
| `secrets` | no | `Secret[]`, default `[]` | Existing install/trust disclosure |
| `dependencies` | no | `RuntimeDependencies`, default empty lists | Python/npm/system runtime disclosure; unrelated to database dependencies |
| `astrid_version` | no | `ReleaseVersion` | Minimum compatible Astrid release metadata; not trust |
| `database` | no | `DatabaseContribution` | Bundled-only SQLite contribution |
| `resources` | no | `SupplementalResource[]`, default `[]` | Runtime/doc assets not reached by another typed declaration |
| `authoring_only` | no | `AuthoringExclusion[]`, default `[]` | Explicit non-wheel classification |

At least one of `content`, `extensions`, `documentation`, `database`, or `resources` must contribute a non-empty value.

The following v1 fields are prohibited: `origin`, `install_tier`, `pack_type`, `metadata`, and `docs`. Provenance replaces the first three; `documentation` replaces `docs`; arbitrary top-level metadata is not retained.

## 3. Nested shapes

### `content`

All keys are optional `RelativeDir` values:

```yaml
content:
  executors: executors
  orchestrators: orchestrators
  elements: elements
  schemas: schemas
  examples: examples
  docs: docs
```

There is no fallback tree scan when a v2 content root is absent. Executor, orchestrator, and element roots use the existing typed component loaders. Schema/example/docs directories expand recursively as declared resources.

### `permissions`

```yaml
permissions:
  - id: subprocess
    reason: Runs ffmpeg.
    access: optional nonblank text
    services: [optional-service-name]
```

- `id` enum: `project_files`, `network`, `subprocess`, `environment`, `accelerator`, `external_services`.
- Exact keys: `id`, `reason`, optional `access`, optional `services`.
- Duplicate permission IDs reject.
- `reason` is required.
- Service names are trimmed, duplicate-rejected, and canonically sorted.

### `aliases`

```yaml
aliases:
  - kind: executor
    alias: rendering.render_video
    canonical_id: rendering.render
```

- Exact keys: `kind`, `alias`, `canonical_id`.
- `kind` enum: `executor`, `orchestrator`, `renderer`, `planner`, `finalizer`.
- Both IDs are `QualifiedId`, distinct, and owned by the declaring pack.
- Duplicate `(kind, alias)` rejects.
- `deprecated` and `deprecation_message` are prohibited.
- Every current deprecated alias is deleted, including the 49 `builtin.*` aliases and any remaining `external.*`/legacy aliases. V2 aliases cannot be compatibility shims.

### `agent`

```yaml
agent:
  purpose: optional prose
  do_not_use_for: optional prose
  normal_entrypoints: [optional trimmed routing strings]
  required_context: [optional/owner-relative.md]
```

Only these four keys exist. `required_context` entries are `RelativeFile` resources and enter the resource closure. Routing strings are descriptive; the typed registries remain authoritative.

### `documentation`

Exactly one tagged shape:

```yaml
documentation:
  kind: skill
  path: skill/SKILL.md
```

```yaml
documentation:
  kind: agents
  path: AGENTS.md
```

```yaml
documentation:
  kind: none
  reason: Non-user-facing internal utility pack.
```

Rules:

- `skill` requires the exact path `skill/SKILL.md`.
- `agents` requires the exact path `AGENTS.md`.
- `none` permits only `kind` and a nonblank `reason`.
- Bundled admission rejects `none` unless the coverage ledger classifies the pack as non-user-facing internal utility.
- For this run, all 22 retained product packs are user/agent-facing and therefore use `skill` or `agents`; there are no product-pack opt-outs.

### `secrets`

```yaml
secrets:
  - name: FAL_KEY
    required: true
    description: optional prose
```

Exact keys are `name`, optional `required` defaulting to `false`, and optional `description`. Names are unique `EnvironmentName` values.

### `dependencies`

```yaml
dependencies:
  python: ["package>=1"]
  npm: ["package@1"]
  system: ["ffmpeg"]
```

Only `python`, `npm`, and `system` exist. Each is a duplicate-free list of trimmed nonblank strings. These remain install/trust disclosures and do not participate in pack or migration solving.

### `resources`

```yaml
resources:
  - path: server/blender-render-api.service
    kind: service
```

Exact keys are `path` and `kind`. `kind` enum:

- `runtime`
- `schema`
- `template`
- `documentation`
- `service`
- `data`

A path may name a file or directory. Directories expand recursively. A physical file may be reached by only one typed or supplemental declaration.

### `authoring_only`

```yaml
authoring_only:
  - path: executors/generate_image/golden
    kind: golden
    reason: Provider demonstration inputs; not required at runtime.
```

Exact keys are `path`, `kind`, and `reason`. `kind` enum:

- `test`
- `golden`
- `development_fixture`
- `authoring_document`
- `build_output`
- `placeholder`

These paths must exist in source, cannot overlap declared resources, and must not occur in the wheel.

### `extensions`

The v1 extension families remain, but string/object dual forms are removed and all nested objects are strict:

```yaml
extensions:
  generation:
    backends:
      - id: backend_id
        label: optional
        module: python.module
        class: ClassName
        init_kwargs: {}        # scoped arbitrary frozen JSON
    features:
      - id: feature_id
        label: optional
        description: optional
    modes:
      - id: mode_id
        label: optional
        description: optional

  elements:
    kinds:
      - id: effects
        singular: optional
        plural: optional
        label: optional
        description: optional

  timeline:
    kinds:
      - catalog: transition  # transition|clip|track
        id: cross_fade
        aliases: []
        default: false

  rendering:
    renderers: [backends/remotion/renderer.yaml]
    planners: [planners/layer_stack/planner.yaml]
    finalizers: [finalizers/ffmpeg/finalizer.yaml]

  schemas: {}                # explicitly scoped arbitrary frozen JSON

  artifact_types:
    types:
      - id: artifact_id
        aliases: []
        description: optional
```

All IDs and paths are duplicate-free. Rendering manifest paths are `RelativeFile` resources and are traversed through the existing typed renderer/planner/finalizer validators.

### `database`

```yaml
database:
  depends_on:
    - pack: core
      min_migration: 1
  migrations:
    - version: 1
      name: initial
      path: migrations/0001_initial.sql
      tables: [timelines]
  stream_types: [timeline.timeline]
  event_kinds: [timeline.created]
  command_kinds: [timeline.create]
  repositories: [TimelineRepository]
  conformance: [replay]
  cli_mounts:
    timelines: timelines
  bridge_mounts: [timelines]
```

All nine fields are required, even when empty:

| Field | Exact type |
|---|---|
| `depends_on` | Unique `{pack: PackId, min_migration: positive integer}[]` |
| `migrations` | Non-empty `Migration[]` |
| `stream_types` | Unique `DottedVocabulary[]` |
| `event_kinds` | Unique `DottedVocabulary[]` |
| `command_kinds` | Unique `DottedVocabulary[]` |
| `repositories` | Unique `RepositoryId[]` |
| `conformance` | Unique `LowerIdent[]` |
| `cli_mounts` | Map `LowerIdent →` one-or-more single-space-separated `LowerIdent` tokens |
| `bridge_mounts` | Unique `LowerIdent[]` |

Each migration contains exactly:

- `version`: positive integer; unique within the pack.
- `name`: `MigrationName`; unique within the pack.
- `path`: `RelativeFile` ending in `.sql`; unique within the pack.
- `tables`: non-empty, unique `LowerIdent[]`; a table may appear in only one migration in the pack and only one database contribution globally.

Migration descriptors must already appear in strictly increasing version order. YAML never contains columns, indexes, constraints, SQL transformations, applied state, checksums, or a mutable head.

## 4. Normalization and immutable model

One load produces a manifest-derived `PackDefinition` containing no root, source, trust, install state, or manifest path.

Normalization is exact:

- Outer whitespace is stripped from prose and disclosure strings.
- Identifiers must already have canonical case; the loader never lowercases them.
- Paths are converted to canonical POSIX relative strings and then to confined resource handles.
- Ordered operational lists preserve declared order only for `agent.normal_entrypoints`.
- Migrations must be declared in increasing version order and retain that order.
- Set-like lists—keywords, capability tags, permissions, services, aliases, dependencies, vocabulary, repositories, conformance, resources, and exclusions—reject duplicates and normalize to deterministic lexical order.
- Mappings normalize by sorted key.
- Arbitrary JSON is permitted only in `extensions.generation.backends[].init_kwargs` and `extensions.schemas`; it is recursively frozen into immutable mappings/tuples.
- No raw manifest mapping survives in the public canonical object.
- `PackDefinition`, all nested declarations, `CatalogEntry`, provenance, resource handles, database projections, and snapshots are frozen/slot-based immutable values.
- Serialization emits canonical keys and lists from normalized values; it never emits loader roots or trust as manifest data.

The catalog envelope owns:

```text
CatalogEntry
├── definition: PackDefinition
├── provenance: CatalogProvenance
├── manifest: ResourceHandle
└── resources: tuple[ResourceHandle, ...]
```

This directly removes the current `PackDefinition.root`, `manifest_path`, self-asserted `origin`, `install_tier`, and `pack_type` mixture.

## 5. Golden and invalid fixtures

Required golden fixtures:

1. Capability-only: executor content, permission, documentation, no database.
2. Database-only: complete database block and documentation, no capability roots.
3. Combined: references-style content/database/SDK/CLI/documentation declaration.

Required invalid fixtures include:

- Missing, string, float, boolean, or non-2 `schema_version`.
- `pack.yml`, `pack.json`, schema-less YAML, flat parser input, arbitrary manifest path, and `schema-pack.yaml`.
- Folder/ID mismatch and bad release version.
- Every unknown top-level and nested field class.
- Every prohibited v1 field.
- Null optional values.
- Duplicate set-like entries.
- Alias ownership violations or deprecated alias fields.
- Malformed permissions and documentation unions.
- Absolute, backslash, empty, traversal, symlink, missing, wrong-kind, or duplicate resource paths.
- Resource/authoring overlap.
- External `database`.
- Missing dependency, dependency on capability-only pack, bad minimum head, self-dependency, cycle, and duplicate dependency.
- Unordered/duplicate migrations, duplicate tables, non-SQL path.
- Provenance spoofing attempts.
- Undeclared bundled non-Python files and wheel-only/source-only resources.

---

# II. Catalog construction, precedence, trust, and lifetime

## 1. Bundled catalog

`BundledCatalog` is built from the installed/source `astrid/packs` package root:

1. Enumerate direct child directories in lexical order.
2. Ignore `_core`, cache directories, and directories without `pack.yaml`.
3. Require exactly one `pack.yaml`; alternate pack filenames are errors.
4. Load, normalize, validate, resolve, classify, and digest each retained bundled pack.
5. Reject duplicate bundled IDs fatally.
6. Cache the resulting immutable catalog once per process.

The empty `builtin` directory is removed, not ignored or represented by a tombstone at runtime.

The bundled catalog cache contains declarations and resources only. It never contains a database connection, service, repository, CLI parser, bridge, executor instance, or application object.

## 2. Dynamic candidate capture

Each top-level operation captures local/external candidates once from the current layers:

1. `local`
2. explicit extra roots, in caller-given root order
3. `ASTRID_PACKS_PATH`, in environment path order
4. installed active revisions, sorted by pack ID

Within a root, candidates are sorted by pack ID and canonical manifest path. Repeated references to the same resolved root and manifest digest are deduplicated.

Read-only discovery never creates `astrid/packs/local`; explicit create/fork operations own that mutation.

## 3. `CatalogSnapshot`

Every top-level operation constructs exactly one immutable snapshot:

```text
CatalogSnapshot
├── bundled_catalog
├── entries_by_id
├── ordered_entries
├── rejected_candidates
├── snapshot_fingerprint
└── construction_context
```

Construction combines the cached bundled catalog with that operation’s dynamic capture using precedence:

```text
bundled > local > explicit-extra > environment > installed
```

Duplicate policy:

- Bundled IDs are reserved and always win.
- An identical resolved root/digest encountered twice is deduplicated.
- Any other later candidate with an already-winning ID is excluded and recorded in `rejected_candidates` as `duplicate_id`, including winner and loser provenance.
- External discovery may continue after an invalid candidate, preserving current fault isolation.
- Direct validation/install of the rejected candidate returns a hard error.
- Lower-precedence candidates never partially contribute aliases, capabilities, documentation, or resources after losing pack identity.

Trust/admission policy:

| Source | Inspect | Capability execution | Database |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| bundled | yes | yes | yes |
| local | yes | yes | no |
| explicit extra | yes | yes | no |
| environment | yes | no | no |
| installed | yes | only active validated revision with accepted permissions | no |

A manifest’s descriptive fields cannot change this table.

When any non-bundled candidate contains `database`, the entire candidate is rejected as `external_database_forbidden` immediately after structural normalization and provenance assignment—before resource resolution, migration SQL reads, alias registration, or typed-registry projection.

## 4. Operation/lifetime invariant

A single snapshot is threaded through every projection used by one operation:

- One CLI invocation.
- One `sdk.discover`, `get_capability`, or `invoke` call.
- One `AstridClient.open()` lifetime.
- One standard application composition/lifetime.
- One serve composition/lifetime.
- One doctor run.
- One inspect command.
- One backup create or restore validation.
- One install validation/admission.
- One package/source audit.

Consumers may not rescan or reconstruct a snapshot internally. Convenience entry points may create a snapshot only when they are themselves the top-level boundary.

An `AstridClient` or standard application holds its snapshot until `close()`. Filesystem/install changes become visible only to the next operation/application.

Every resource handle records its owner-relative path, resolved root, file kind, size, and SHA-256 at snapshot construction. Reads verify the recorded digest. If an external revision changes during the operation, the handle raises `CatalogSnapshotStaleError`; the consumer never reparses or silently adopts changed bytes.

Existing registries accept `CatalogSnapshot` or the narrow projection derived from it. No registry, SDK service, renderer loader, skill index, doctor, backup, or inspect path calls discovery independently.

---

# III. Bounded bundled-resource classification and audit

The resource gate is deliberately closed-world and does not attempt perfect static analysis.

## 1. Inventory boundary

For each bundled pack root:

1. Walk deterministically without following symlinks.
2. Reject every symlink encountered.
3. Inventory every regular file except `.py` source files and cache/build metadata already prohibited by layout validation.
4. Treat `pack.yaml` as the implicit canonical-manifest resource.
5. Require every other inventoried file to have exactly one classification:

   - reached through a typed declaration;
   - reached through one supplemental `resources` entry; or
   - covered by one `authoring_only` entry.

Zero classifications is `undeclared_file`. More than one is `duplicate_classification`.

## 2. Typed reachability

Typed seeds and edges are exact:

- `content.executors`, `orchestrators`, and `elements` locate component roots through existing typed component validators; they do not declare every file below those roots wholesale.
- Component manifests, their schema-declared path fields, their documented `STAGE.md` convention, declared assets, and declared schema/doc routes become resource edges.
- `content.schemas`, `content.examples`, and `content.docs` recursively classify their directories.
- Rendering extension lists classify their renderer/planner/finalizer manifests; existing typed validators enumerate their path-valued fields.
- `database.migrations[].path` classifies SQL.
- `documentation.path` and `agent.required_context` classify documentation.
- `resources[].path` classifies a supplemental file or recursively expands a directory.
- Nested component `skill/SKILL.md`, requirements files, templates, service units, and other opaque assets must be reached by a typed component declaration or listed explicitly under `resources`.

Directories expand in lexical relative-path order. Empty declared directories reject.

## 3. Targeted runtime-use AST check

Scan only runtime `.py` files under bundled pack roots, excluding paths classified as test, golden, development fixture, or build output.

The checker recognizes:

- `open(...)`
- `Path.open`, `read_text`, `read_bytes`
- `importlib.resources.files`, `open_text`, `open_binary`
- `pkgutil.get_data`
- simple `Path(__file__)`/`__file__`-derived constants and `/`, `joinpath`, `with_name`, or `parent` literal path composition
- the existing executor-folder convention that probes `requirements*.txt`
- the Blender deployment service-unit lookup
- existing typed component/rendering manifest resource APIs

It performs only intra-module constant propagation for simple literal assignments. Arbitrary user-supplied paths, generated outputs, network inputs, and dynamic project paths are not classified as pack resources.

Any statically resolvable pack-relative read must resolve to the declared closure. A statically resolvable read outside the pack root must either:

- move the asset into its owning pack; or
- resolve through an explicitly kernel-owned resource recorded in the coverage ledger.

It cannot be papered over with an authoring exclusion.

Known runtime filenames—migration SQL, component manifests, `STAGE.md`, `SKILL.md`, `requirements*.txt`, referenced schemas/templates, and `.service` files—cannot use an authoring-only classification.

## 4. Source/wheel equality

The source audit emits the declared closure as sorted records:

```json
{
  "pack_id": "blender",
  "path": "server/blender-render-api.service",
  "kind": "service",
  "size": 1234,
  "sha256": "..."
}
```

The clean-wheel audit reconstructs the bundled catalog in a source-isolated process and emits the same records.

Required equality:

- Same pack IDs.
- Same normalized manifest definitions.
- Same relative resource paths.
- Same resource kinds, sizes, and SHA-256 values.
- Same database declarations and derived heads.
- Same documentation routes.
- Same bundled provenance class.

Absolute roots are intentionally different and are not compared.

The reverse audit also fails if the wheel contains an undeclared bundled non-Python file or any `authoring_only` file.

Use ordinary setuptools package-data configuration; do not add a packaging backend.

---

# IV. Concrete audit-only coverage ledger

## 1. Files and commands

Committed reviewed ledger:

```text
docs/contracts/canonical-pack-coverage.json
```

Implementation:

```text
scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py
```

Boundary commands:

```bash
python3 scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py inventory \
  --root . \
  --output .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/inventory.json

python3 scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py check \
  --inventory .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/inventory.json \
  --ledger docs/contracts/canonical-pack-coverage.json \
  --output .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/check.json

python3 scripts/canonical_pack_coverage.py diff \
  --before .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<previous>/inventory.json \
  --after .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/inventory.json \
  --output .oracle/evidence/canonical-pack/<boundary>/diff.json
```

`check` exits nonzero for any issue. Runtime code never imports the script, ledger, inventory, or diff.

## 2. Canonical inventory inputs

The inventory command reads only:

- The canonical bundled `CatalogSnapshot`.
- Typed executor, orchestrator, element, renderer, planner, and finalizer projections.
- The canonical database projection.
- The resource-closure result.
- Structured documentation routes.
- Code-declared kernel migration/vocabulary/repository constants.
- Exact AST symbol inspection of the existing application composition, SDK service fields, product/operational CLI declarations, bridge mounts, doctor, backup, restore, inspect, package audit, and agent-census generator.

It does not use the reviewed ledger to discover surfaces and does not broadly grep arbitrary text.

## 3. Inventory surface IDs

Exact forms:

```text
pack:<pack_id>
executor:<qualified_id>
orchestrator:<qualified_id>
element:<kind>/<id>
renderer:<qualified_id>
planner:<qualified_id>
finalizer:<qualified_id>
alias:<kind>:<qualified_id>
migration:<pack_id>/<integer>
table:<table>
stream_type:<dotted_name>
event_kind:<dotted_name>
command_kind:<dotted_name>
repository:<RepositoryId>
conformance:<pack_id>/<dimension>
cli_mount:<space-separated path>
sdk_surface:<stable service or method id>
bridge_mount:<token>
runtime_resource:<pack_id>/<relative path>
agent_document:<pack_id>/<relative path>
operational_consumer:<application|doctor|backup|restore|inspect|package|agent_census>
```

Rows are sorted by `(kind, surface_id)`. Evidence paths and consumers are unique and sorted.

## 4. Ledger schema

```json
{
  "schema": "astrid.canonical_pack_coverage.v1",
  "surfaces": [
    {
      "surface_id": "table:timelines",
      "kind": "table",
      "disposition": "active",
      "owner": "pack:timeline",
      "evidence_paths": [
        "astrid/packs/timeline/pack.yaml",
        "astrid/packs/timeline/migrations/0001_initial.sql"
      ],
      "projection_consumers": [
        "operational_consumer:application",
        "operational_consumer:doctor"
      ]
    },
    {
      "surface_id": "alias:executor:builtin.render",
      "kind": "alias",
      "disposition": "deleted",
      "owner": null,
      "justification": "Deprecated compatibility alias removed by the beta hard cut.",
      "evidence_paths": [
        "baseline:7ac50c12e8e4d90988fee603ffdb9896e5628792:astrid/packs/rendering/pack.yaml"
      ],
      "projection_consumers": []
    }
  ]
}
```

Rules:

- `disposition` is `active` or `deleted`.
- Active owner is exactly `pack:<PackId>` or `kernel:<LowerIdent>`.
- Deleted rows have `owner: null`, a nonblank justification, immutable baseline evidence, and no consumers.
- Pack-owned rows must agree with the owner derived from their canonical catalog/database/resource projection. A manual override is impossible.
- Kernel rows require a nonblank justification, at least one implementation evidence path, at least one test/contract evidence path, and must not be representable by an existing pack declaration.
- Every active row has at least one resolvable evidence path and one valid consumer unless its kind is itself `operational_consumer`.
- Every consumer reference must resolve to another active inventory row.
- `builtin` and every deprecated alias are deletion records, never synthetic pack or kernel ownership.
- The 49 known `builtin.*` aliases are an exact baseline assertion; WP0 additionally records and deletes every other deprecated alias discovered at the pinned source.

## 5. Exact comparisons

`check` emits and fails on:

- `missing`: inventory surface has no ledger row.
- `stale`: active ledger row is absent from inventory or has missing evidence.
- `duplicate`: repeated surface ID or repeated classification.
- `conflict`: ledger owner differs from the mechanically derived owner, or inventory derives multiple owners.
- `deleted_still_active`: a deleted ledger ID is present in inventory.
- `invalid_kernel_justification`: missing implementation/test evidence or pack-representable ownership.
- `invalid_consumer`: missing, deleted, or cyclic self-only consumer reference.
- `unclassified`: inventory row has no valid active owner.
- `undeclared_resource`: resource audit failure.
- `unexpected_owner`: pack referenced by owner is absent from the bundled catalog.

Boundary diff records exact added, removed, changed-owner, changed-evidence, changed-consumer, and changed-resource-digest sets. Reviewer approval is required for every non-empty category.

WP0’s immutable baseline inventory is evidence only. It is never imported by final code or treated as current truth.

---

# V. Deterministic database composition and migration semantics

## 1. Projection nodes

The database graph contains:

- One synthetic code-declared `core` node, head `1`, using existing core SQL and vocabulary.
- Every `CatalogSnapshot` entry whose provenance is bundled and whose definition contains `database`.

No pack ID allowlist or standard tuple participates.

Every selected node contains:

- ID.
- Immutable database declaration.
- Owner resource handles.
- Derived migration head.
- Provenance.
- Existing collision projection.

A synthetic trusted bundled database pack added to a test snapshot must enter composition without changing composition code.

## 2. Migration head and dependency meaning

For node `P`:

```text
head(P) = max(P.migrations[].version)
```

`depends_on: {pack: Q, min_migration: N}` means:

- `Q` must be a selected database node or `core`.
- `head(Q) >= N`.
- All migrations of `Q` precede every migration of `P`.
- It does not compare pack release versions.
- It does not solve ranges.
- It does not inspect project-applied state during graph construction.

Validation before opening a database:

- Every database pack has a direct or transitive dependency path to `core`.
- No dependency targets the declaring pack.
- No duplicate target.
- No target with no database migrations.
- Every minimum is a positive integer no greater than the target head.
- The graph is acyclic.
- All current table, migration identity/name, vocabulary, repository, CLI, and bridge collision classes remain rejected before database open.

## 3. Exact standard graph

After conversion:

```text
core@1
├── references@1  requires core >= 1
├── runaway@1     requires core >= 1
├── shots@1       requires core >= 1
└── timeline@1    requires core >= 1
```

The pack release versions remain strings such as `1.0.0`; they do not enter this graph.

## 4. Ordering and tie-break

Use deterministic Kahn topological sorting:

1. Create an edge `dependency → dependent`.
2. Seed a min-heap with every zero-indegree node.
3. Heap key is `(0 if id == "core" else 1, pack_id)`.
4. Pop the lowest key, append it, decrement dependents, and push newly ready nodes using the same key.
5. If fewer nodes are emitted than selected, return the lexical cycle path as an error.
6. Within a node, emit migrations by increasing integer version.

The current graph therefore orders:

```text
core/1
references/1
runaway/1
shots/1
timeline/1
```

Before runaway is present, the retained order is:

```text
core/1
references/1
shots/1
timeline/1
```

On a current three-pack database, those existing keys are skipped and only `runaway/1` is applied. Existing `schema_migrations` rows are not rewritten or resequenced.

## 5. Preserved migration identity and safety

For all current migrations preserve exactly:

- `pack`.
- Integer `version`.
- `name` (`initial`).
- SQL bytes.
- Owned tables.
- SHA-256 computation.
- Name/checksum drift checks.
- Too-new and unknown-pack refusal.
- Per-migration `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.
- DDL/DML and migration-row atomicity.
- Read-only compatibility probing.
- `schema_migrations` as the sole applied-state record.

Replace `pack_resource_root(pack_id)` with the registered owner `ResourceHandle`; the runner never reconstructs `astrid/packs/<id>`.

Core remains explicit code-owned kernel behavior.

---

# VI. Documentation, census, and per-pack preservation

- All 22 retained product packs declare direct structured docs.
- Add direct skills for blender, timeline, shots, references, and runaway.
- Repair media’s frontmatter.
- Package all declared pack and nested component skills.
- `_core` remains a skill-only kernel shell with no `pack.yaml`.
- Extend the existing generator so one snapshot produces both the capability index and canonical pack census.
- Generated census fields: ID, name, contribution categories, capability counts, database tables/head, documentation route, and ownership link.
- The census includes neither DDL nor applied database state.
- Check mode fails on drift.
- Delete independent pack-skill inventory and raw directory reparsing; skills consume snapshot documentation/component projections.

Maintain the four-pack preservation matrix for timeline, shots, references, and runaway across:

- migration;
- repository;
- events;
- commands;
- CLI;
- SDK;
- bridge;
- conformance.

Every cell has baseline and final evidence or an evidence-backed `N/A`. Broad suite success cannot replace the matrix.
